- Power counting in $3+1$ and $7{+}1$ (and $4{+}4$) dimensions, the cutoff dependence of the contact coupling, the divergence structure of the one-loop bubble of the Yukawa completion, the finite list of its couplings, and the equality of loop invariance and closure invariance for quadratic forms.

### B. Not established

- No quantum theory on a Hilbert space has the super-loop as a symmetry, and none can within ordinary quantum mechanics (Theorem R). Whether a non-associative quantum mechanics, such as the octonionic one of Günaydin, Piron and Ruegg ([Günaydin, Piron, and Ruegg 1978](#ref-GunaydinPironRuegg1978)), can carry it was not studied. The dynamical hypotheses H-dyn of the theorems on the scattering matrix are therefore not tested.

- The counterexample does not extend to the Poincaré algebra: with translations present no Malcev algebra of our families lets spinor charges close into Lorentz generators (Theorem M(c)). Larger families—more internal generators, central or tensorial charges, other representations of the spinor charges—were not classified.

- The field $\Psi_{\rm LL}$ of the superfluid is a fermion field by the spin-statistics theorem; it carries the quantum numbers of the spinor charges, but the dynamics of Eq. (24) is an ordinary relativistic field theory with the Lie symmetries $SL(2,\mathbb C)\times SU(2)_{\rm v}\times
  U(1)^{\rm F}_{\rm LL}$, chosen by us. The name “electric charge” for $T^3_{\rm LL}$ is our choice (a hypercharge could be added).

- The superfluid was not treated beyond mean field: no fluctuation corrections, no finite temperature, no dispersion of the Goldstone modes, no other interactions.

- Renormalizability is decided by power counting and by the one-loop divergence structure of the Yukawa completion, not by a renormalization to all orders.

## XII. Conclusions

Every proof of the no-go theorems treats symmetries as associative objects: a group, operators on a space of states, generators with a Jacobi identity. Dropping associativity, and only that, the unit bi-octonions give a structure in which the Lorentz group and internal charges are united: the Lorentz super-loop $\mathbb L_{\rm LL}$, a Moufang loop that contains $SL(2,\mathbb C)$ and eight spinor charges, a Weyl spinor that is an isospin doublet with electric charges $\pm\tfrac12$, whose brackets close into the Lorentz generators. No Lie algebra can do this (Theorem L); among all covariant brackets the super-loop is the only Malcev algebra that does (Theorem M); and its non-associativity is essential, because it has no realization by operators (Theorem R). The super-loop carries operators with the eigenvalues of electric charge, isospin, spin, chirality and spinor number, and a new one, the twist charge, whose value $-2$ on the spinor charges measures how far the Lorentz action on them is from a representation. It realizes the inversion of the Lorentz group as an element, which no group can. It lives on the $7{+}1$ and $3+1$ Minkowski spaces inside the bi-octonions, on the $4{+}4$ split octonions, and on itself, a parallelizable manifold without a group structure. The spin-zero superfluid of its spinor charges condenses in an isospin-triplet, unitary, electrically neutral state, and the pair that is a singlet of the whole super-loop requires two species. In $3+1$ dimensions its Yukawa form is renormalizable; on the eight-dimensional manifolds it is not.

## Data Availability

The data that support the findings of this article are openly available in the repository of PROJECT spacetimes, folder `Revision_SuperLoop`, at <https://github.com/once-ere/spacetimes>: the programs, every data file, the reports of all machine checks, the figures and the sources of this paper (Appendix C).

## Appendix A: Notation

Table III defines every symbol of this paper, by group, with the plain-text names under which the programs, the figures and the data files write it. Two entries are taken from the master registries of the symbols of PROJECT spacetimes and keep exactly the meaning that they have in the rest of the project, followed by a note on their use here: the index $j$ (registered in the project as $i,j$, the index of the space-like coordinates; here only $j$ is an index, and $i$ is the imaginary unit) and the number $D$ of space dimensions. All other entries are new. Every new symbol carries the suffix LL (for *Lorentz super-loop*), written as a subscript ($Q_{\rm LL}$) or, where the lower position holds an index, as a superscript ($\sigma^{\rm LL}_j$), except the following, which cannot be read as anything else: the bi-octonions and their parts in bold upright type ($\mathbf x$, $\mathbf
e_p$, $\mathbf u$, $\mathbf b$, …), the unit matrix $\mathsf 1_4$ and the operators $\mathsf O_1$, $\mathsf O_2$, $\mathsf O_3$ of Sec. II, the number sets and algebras in blackboard type ($\mathbb R$, $\mathbb C$, $\mathbb H$, $\mathbb O$), the groups and Lie algebras ($SL(2,\mathbb C)$, $\mathfrak{so}(3,1)$, …), operation signs, and the plain symbols $i$ (the imaginary unit), $p$ (an index over the basis of an algebra) and $\iota$ (the isospin index). The letter $\mathsf K$ without scripts in Theorem SF1 is any $8\times8$ pair matrix.

The machine checks LL-S1 to LL-S8 (Appendix B) verify that the symbols obey these rules: no symbol and no plain-text name is registered twice (LL-S1); the taken entries carry the meanings of the master registries, no new symbol coincides with a symbol of a master registry, and the symbols that this paper shares with the sibling folder `Revision_NoGo` of the repository are declared and denote the same objects there (LL-S2); every column of every data file is a registered name or a descriptive word (LL-S3); every document of this revision contains the complete table of symbols (LL-S4); the symbols of the superfluid, $\mu_{\rm LL}$, $\Lambda_{\rm LL}$, $\Omega_{\rm LL}$, $\nu^{\rm F}_{\rm LL}$, $\Delta_{\rm LL}$, $\zeta_{\rm LL}$, $\varepsilon_{\rm LL}$, always carry their suffix (LL-S5); no letter that the project uses with another meaning occurs in a formula without the suffix LL (LL-S6); every letter in bold, sans-serif, calligraphic, blackboard or fraktur type is registered (LL-S7); and every name with the suffix `_LL`, every name of an algebra element, every coefficient and every pair matrix that occurs in a report or data file is registered (LL-S8). Units: $\hbar$ and the speed of light are 1; in Secs. IX and X the momentum cutoff $\Lambda_{\rm LL}=1$ unless a unit is shown.

| $D_{\rm LL}(\mathbf u)$, $D^{\rm v}_{\rm LL}(\mathbf t)$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | the Lorentz and isospin derivations of $\mathbb B_{\rm LL}$: $D_{\rm LL}(\mathbf u)(\mathbf a+\mathbf b\mathbf e_4)=[\mathbf u,\mathbf a]+(-\mathbf b\mathbf u)\mathbf e_4$ and $D^{\rm v}_{\rm LL}(\mathbf t)(\mathbf a+\mathbf b\mathbf e_4)=(\mathbf t\mathbf b)\mathbf e_4$ (both linear in their argument)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | `D_LL` `Dv_LL`                                                                                                                                                                                          |

| $\varphi^{\rm LL}_{\mathbf g,\mathbf v}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | the automorphism $\mathbf a+\mathbf b\mathbf e_4\mapsto\mathbf g\mathbf a\bar{\mathbf g}+(\mathbf v\mathbf b\bar{\mathbf g})\mathbf e_4$ of $\mathbb B_{\rm LL}$ ($\mathbf g$ a Lorentz transformation, $\mathbf v$ an isospin rotation)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | `varphi`                                                                                                                                                                                                |

| $\mathbb O^{\rm s}_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | the split octonions inside $\mathbb B_{\rm LL}$: the real space $\mathbb H+(i\mathbb H)\mathbf e_4$ (real combinations of $\mathbf e_0,\dots,\mathbf e_3,i\mathbf e_4,\dots,i\mathbf e_7$), closed under the product, on which $N_{\rm LL}$ has signature $(4,4)$: the $4{+}4$ manifold (plain text `R^(4,4)`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | `Os` `R^(4,4)`                                                                                                                                                                                          |

| $\mathsf L_{\rm LL}(\mathbf g)$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | the real $4\times4$ matrix of the Lorentz transformation $\mathbf x\mapsto\mathbf g\mathbf x\mathbf g^\dagger$ of $\mathbb R^{1,3}_{\rm LL}$ in the coordinates $x^0_{\rm LL},\dots,x^3_{\rm LL}$, for a unit bi-quaternion $\mathbf g$: its entry in row $p$ and column $p'$ ($p,p'=0,\dots,3$) is $\tfrac12\,\mathrm{Re}\,\mathrm{tr}(\sigma^{\rm LL}_pM_{\rm LL}(\mathbf g)\,\sigma^{\rm LL}_{p'}M_{\rm LL}(\mathbf g)^\dagger)$, with $\sigma^{\rm LL}_0$ the unit matrix                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | `Lmat`                                                                                                                                                                                                  |

| $\mathsf D_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | the isospin part of the pairing block of the scalar channel, the $2\times2$ matrix $\mathsf D_{\rm LL}=\sum_\iota d^\iota_{\rm LL}(\mathsf E_{\rm LL}\tau^{\rm LL}_\iota)^\dagger$, so that $\mathsf P_{\rm LL}=(C_{\rm LL}\gamma^{\rm ch}_{\rm LL})^\dagger\otimes\mathsf D_{\rm LL}$; it is also the isospin wavefunction of the condensed pairs (rows and columns: isospin up, down); plain text `Dpair` (not `D_LL`, which is the Lorentz derivation)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | `Dpair`                                                                                                                                                                                                 |

| **9 Test 2: the gauge-covariant field equations of the spinor field and their solutions**                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| $\eta^{\rm LL}_{pp'}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | the Minkowski metric of $\mathbb R^{1,3}_{\rm LL}$ in the coordinates $x^p_{\rm LL}$: $\eta^{\rm LL}_{00}=1$, $\eta^{\rm LL}_{11}=\eta^{\rm LL}_{22}=\eta^{\rm LL}_{33}=-1$ and $\eta^{\rm LL}_{pp'}=0$ for $p\neq p'$; it is its own inverse and raises and lowers the indices $p$, $p'$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | `eta`                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| $x_{\rm LL}$, $\vec x_{\rm LL}$; $\partial_p$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | a point of $\mathbb R^{1,3}_{\rm LL}$ given by its coordinates, $x_{\rm LL}=(x^0_{\rm LL},x^1_{\rm LL},x^2_{\rm LL},x^3_{\rm LL})$ (its Hermitian bi-quaternion is $x^0_{\rm LL}\mathbf e_0+i\sum_jx^j_{\rm LL}\mathbf e_j$), and its space part $\vec x_{\rm LL}=(x^1_{\rm LL},x^2_{\rm LL},x^3_{\rm LL})$; $x^0_{\rm LL}$ is the time; $\partial_p$ is the partial derivative with respect to $x^p_{\rm LL}$ (plain text `d_p`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | `x_LL` `xvec` `d_p`                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| $\mathbf f_p$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | the frame of $\mathbb R^{1,3}_{\rm LL}$: four Hermitian bi-quaternions $\mathbf f_0,\dots,\mathbf f_3$ with $N_{\rm LL}(\mathbf f_p,\mathbf f_{p'})=\eta^{\rm LL}_{pp'}$, possibly depending on the point $x_{\rm LL}$ (a vierbein); the canonical frame is $\mathbf f_0=\mathbf e_0$, $\mathbf f_j=i\,\mathbf e_j$, for which $M_{\rm LL}(\mathbf f_p)=\sigma^{\rm LL}_p$, $M_{\rm LL}(\bar{\mathbf f}_0)=\sigma^{\rm LL}_0$ and $M_{\rm LL}(\bar{\mathbf f}_j)=-\sigma^{\rm LL}_j$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | `f_p` `f_0` `f_j`                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| $\gamma^p_{\rm LL}[\mathbf f]$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | the Dirac matrices of a frame, the $4\times4$ matrices with the $2\times2$ blocks $\gamma^p_{\rm LL}[\mathbf f]=\begin{pmatrix}0&M_{\rm LL}(\mathbf f_p)\\M_{\rm LL}(\bar{\mathbf f}_p)&0\end{pmatrix}$; they satisfy $\gamma^p_{\rm LL}[\mathbf f]\gamma^{p'}_{\rm LL}[\mathbf f]+\gamma^{p'}_{\rm LL}[\mathbf f]\gamma^p_{\rm LL}[\mathbf f]=2N_{\rm LL}(\mathbf f_p,\mathbf f_{p'})\,\mathsf 1_4$; for the canonical frame they are the matrices $\gamma^p_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | `gamma_LL^p[f]`                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| $\tilde{\mathbf g}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | for a unit bi-quaternion $\mathbf g$: $\tilde{\mathbf g}=(\mathbf g^\dagger)^{-1}$, which equals the complex conjugate $\mathbf g^*$; $M_{\rm LL}(\tilde{\mathbf g})=(M_{\rm LL}(\mathbf g)^\dagger)^{-1}$; the change of representation of the Dirac matrices by $\mathbf g$ changes the frame by $\mathbf f_p\mapsto\tilde{\mathbf g}\,\mathbf f_p\,\tilde{\mathbf g}^\dagger$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | `gLt` `gLt^dag`                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| $\mathsf S_{\rm LL}(\mathbf g)$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | the spinor matrix of the Lorentz transformation $\mathbf g$: the $4\times4$ matrix $\mathsf S_{\rm LL}(\mathbf g)=\mathrm{diag}(M_{\rm LL}(\tilde{\mathbf g}),M_{\rm LL}(\mathbf g))$ of $2\times2$ blocks (on the eight components of $\Psi_{\rm LL}$: $\mathsf S_{\rm LL}(\mathbf g)\otimes\sigma^{\rm LL}_0$, plain text `Sspin`); $\mathsf S_{\rm LL}(\mathbf g)^{-1}\gamma^p_{\rm LL}\mathsf S_{\rm LL}(\mathbf g)=\sum_{p'}[\mathsf L_{\rm LL}(\mathbf g)]_{pp'}\gamma^{p'}_{\rm LL}$, where $[\mathsf L_{\rm LL}(\mathbf g)]_{pp'}$ is the entry in row $p$ and column $p'$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | `Sspin_LL` `Sspin`                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| $\mathsf s_{\rm LL}(\mathbf u)$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | a Lorentz generator $\mathbf u$ of $\mathfrak l_{\rm LL}$ acting on the Dirac spinor: the $4\times4$ matrix $\mathsf s_{\rm LL}(\mathbf u)=\mathrm{diag}(-M_{\rm LL}(\mathbf u)^\dagger,M_{\rm LL}(\mathbf u))$, the derivative of $\mathsf S_{\rm LL}$ at the unit; $\mathsf s_{\rm LL}(J^j_{\rm LL})=\tfrac12\gamma^{j'}_{\rm LL}\gamma^{j''}_{\rm LL}$ for cyclic $(j,j',j'')$ and $\mathsf s_{\rm LL}(K^j_{\rm LL})=\tfrac12\gamma^0_{\rm LL}\gamma^j_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | `sgen_LL`                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| $\mathbf b^+_{\rm LL}$, $\mathbf b^-_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | the two spinor charges $\mathbf b^\pm_{\rm LL}\mathbf e_4$ of which the Dirac isodoublet is made: the upper block (chirality $-1$) of $\Psi_{\rm LL}$ is $\psi'_{\rm LL}(\mathbf b^-_{\rm LL})$ and the lower block (chirality $+1$) is $\psi'_{\rm LL}(\mathbf b^+_{\rm LL})$, where $\psi'_{\rm LL}(\mathbf y)=\mathsf E_{\rm LL}^{-1}M_{\rm LL}(\mathbf y)^T$ for a bi-quaternion $\mathbf y$; $\Psi_{\rm LL}[\mathbf b^-_{\rm LL},\mathbf b^+_{\rm LL}]$ is $\Psi_{\rm LL}$ written with them (plain text `Psi(bSm, bSp)`); under the gauge group $\mathbf b^+_{\rm LL}\mapsto\exp(i\theta^{\rm F}_{\rm LL})\,\mathbf v\,\mathbf b^+_{\rm LL}\,\bar{\mathbf g}$, the automorphism $\varphi^{\rm LL}_{\mathbf g,\mathbf v}$ on the spinor charge times a phase, and $\mathbf b^-_{\rm LL}\mapsto\exp(i\theta^{\rm F}_{\rm LL})\,\mathbf v\,\mathbf b^-_{\rm LL}\,\mathbf g^\dagger$, its complex conjugate                                                                                                                                                                                                         | `bSp` `bSm`                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| $\theta^{\rm F}_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | the phase of a local transformation of the fermion number, $\Psi_{\rm LL}\mapsto\exp(i\theta^{\rm F}_{\rm LL})\Psi_{\rm LL}$, a real function of $x_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | `thF`                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| $\mathsf U_{\rm LL}$, $\mathsf R^{\rm v}_{\rm LL}(\mathsf U_{\rm LL})$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | a local isospin rotation: a matrix $\mathsf U_{\rm LL}$ of $SU(2)$, a function of $x_{\rm LL}$, that acts on the isospin index of $\Psi_{\rm LL}$ ($\mathsf U_{\rm LL}=M_{\rm LL}(\mathbf v)$ for a unit real quaternion $\mathbf v$); $\mathsf R^{\rm v}_{\rm LL}(\mathsf U_{\rm LL})$ is the real orthogonal $3\times3$ matrix with $\mathsf U_{\rm LL}\tau^{\rm LL}_\iota\mathsf U_{\rm LL}^\dagger=\sum_{\iota'}[\mathsf R^{\rm v}_{\rm LL}(\mathsf U_{\rm LL})]_{\iota'\iota}\tau^{\rm LL}_{\iota'}$, the rotation of an isospin vector                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | `U_LL` `U_LL^dag` `Rv`                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| $\mathcal U_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | a local gauge transformation of $\Psi_{\rm LL}$ by the gauge group $SL(2,\mathbb C)\times SU(2)_{\rm v}\times U(1)^{\rm F}_{\rm LL}$: $\mathcal U_{\rm LL}=\exp(i\theta^{\rm F}_{\rm LL})\,\mathsf S_{\rm LL}(\mathbf g)\otimes\mathsf U_{\rm LL}$, with $\mathbf g$, $\mathsf U_{\rm LL}$ and $\theta^{\rm F}_{\rm LL}$ functions of $x_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | `Ucal`                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| $A^\iota_{{\rm LL},p}$, $A^{\rm F}_{{\rm LL},p}$; $g^{\rm v}_{\rm LL}$, $g^{\rm F}_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | the gauge potentials, real fields on $\mathbb R^{1,3}_{\rm LL}$: $A^\iota_{{\rm LL},p}$ of the isospin group $SU(2)_{\rm v}$ (three fields for each direction $p$) and $A^{\rm F}_{{\rm LL},p}$ of the fermion number $U(1)^{\rm F}_{\rm LL}$; $g^{\rm v}_{\rm LL}$ and $g^{\rm F}_{\rm LL}$ are their couplings (real numbers); a constant $g^{\rm F}_{\rm LL}A^{\rm F}_{{\rm LL},0}$ acts like a chemical potential                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | `Av_LL` `Av_LL^iota` `Av_LL^iota’` `Av_LL^iota”` `AF_LL` `g_v` `g_F`                                                                                                                                    |
| $\mathsf a_{{\rm LL},p}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | the isospin potential as a Hermitian $2\times2$ matrix on the isospin index, $\mathsf a_{{\rm LL},p}=\sum_\iota A^\iota_{{\rm LL},p}\tau^{\rm LL}_\iota/2$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | `atau_LL`                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| $\mathbf t^{\rm A}_{{\rm LL},p}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | the isospin potential as a real imaginary quaternion, $\mathbf t^{\rm A}_{{\rm LL},p}=\sum_\iota A^\iota_{{\rm LL},p}\mathbf e_\iota/2$ (an element of ${\rm Im}\,\mathbb H$, like $\mathbf t$), with $M_{\rm LL}(\mathbf t^{\rm A}_{{\rm LL},p})=-i\,\mathsf a_{{\rm LL},p}$; it acts on the spinor charges through the isospin derivation $D^{\rm v}_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | `tA` `tA_p`                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| $\mathbf u^\omega_{{\rm LL},p}$; $\omega^j_{{\rm LL},p}$, $\varpi^j_{{\rm LL},p}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | the Lorentz connection: for each direction $p$ an element of $\mathfrak l_{\rm LL}$, $\mathbf u^\omega_{{\rm LL},p}=\sum_j(\omega^j_{{\rm LL},p}J^j_{\rm LL}+\varpi^j_{{\rm LL},p}K^j_{\rm LL})$, with the real rotation components $\omega^j_{{\rm LL},p}$ and the real boost components $\varpi^j_{{\rm LL},p}$; $\mathbf u^{\omega*}_{{\rm LL},p}$ is its complex conjugate (plain text `uLw_p^*`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | `uLw` `uLw_p` `uLw^*` `omega_LL` `varpi_LL`                                                                                                                                                             |
| $\Gamma^{\rm LL}_p$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | the spin connection, the $8\times8$ matrix $\Gamma^{\rm LL}_p=\mathsf s_{\rm LL}(\mathbf u^\omega_{{\rm LL},p})\otimes\sigma^{\rm LL}_0$ (it acts on the Dirac index and leaves the isospin index alone)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | `Gamma_LL`                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| $\mathsf A_{{\rm LL},p}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | the gauge matrix of the internal symmetries on the eight components of $\Psi_{\rm LL}$, $\mathsf A_{{\rm LL},p}=g^{\rm v}_{\rm LL}\sum_\iota A^\iota_{{\rm LL},p}T^\iota_{\rm LL}+g^{\rm F}_{\rm LL}A^{\rm F}_{{\rm LL},p}\mathsf 1_8$, Hermitian ($\mathsf 1_8$ because the fermion number of $\Psi_{\rm LL}$ is 1)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | `Acal` `Acal_0` `Acal_j` `Acal_p`                                                                                                                                                                       |
| $\nabla^{\rm LL}_p$, $\nabla^{{\rm LL}*}_p$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | the gauge-covariant derivative of $\Psi_{\rm LL}$, the $8\times8$ matrix of differential operators $\nabla^{\rm LL}_p=\partial_p+\Gamma^{\rm LL}_p-i\,\mathsf A_{{\rm LL},p}$, and the covariant derivative of a field that transforms like $\Psi^*_{\rm LL}$ (the hole component), $\nabla^{{\rm LL}*}_p=\partial_p+(\Gamma^{\rm LL}_p)^*+i\,(\mathsf A_{{\rm LL},p})^*$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | `nabla_LL` `nabla*_LL`                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| $\mathbf D_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | the bi-quaternion of covariant derivatives, $\mathbf D_{\rm LL}=\sum_p\mathbf f_p\nabla^{\rm LL}_p$ (bold upright like every bi-quaternion of this folder; it is not the Lorentz derivation $D_{\rm LL}(\mathbf u)$, which is italic and has an argument), with $\sum_p\gamma^p_{\rm LL}\nabla^{\rm LL}_p=\begin{pmatrix}0&M_{\rm LL}(\mathbf D_{\rm LL})\\M_{\rm LL}(\bar{\mathbf D}_{\rm LL})&0\end{pmatrix}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | `Dbq_LL`                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| $\mathsf F^\iota_{{\rm LL},pp'}$, $\mathsf F^{\rm F}_{{\rm LL},pp'}$, $\mathsf R^{\rm LL}_{pp'}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | the field strengths and the curvature: $\mathsf F^\iota_{{\rm LL},pp'}=\partial_pA^\iota_{{\rm LL},p'}-\partial_{p'}A^\iota_{{\rm LL},p}+g^{\rm v}_{\rm LL}\sum_{\iota',\iota''}\epsilon^{\rm LL}_{\iota\iota'\iota''}A^{\iota'}_{{\rm LL},p}A^{\iota''}_{{\rm LL},p'}$, $\mathsf F^{\rm F}_{{\rm LL},pp'}=\partial_pA^{\rm F}_{{\rm LL},p'}-\partial_{p'}A^{\rm F}_{{\rm LL},p}$, and the $8\times8$ curvature of the spin connection, $\mathsf R^{\rm LL}_{pp'}=\partial_p\Gamma^{\rm LL}_{p'}-\partial_{p'}\Gamma^{\rm LL}_p+\Gamma^{\rm LL}_p\Gamma^{\rm LL}_{p'}-\Gamma^{\rm LL}_{p'}\Gamma^{\rm LL}_p$; the commutator of two covariant derivatives is $\mathsf R^{\rm LL}_{pp'}-i\,g^{\rm v}_{\rm LL}\sum_\iota\mathsf F^\iota_{{\rm LL},pp'}T^\iota_{\rm LL}-i\,g^{\rm F}_{\rm LL}\mathsf F^{\rm F}_{{\rm LL},pp'}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | `Fv_LL` `FF_LL` `Rc_LL`                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| $\Psi_{{\rm LL},1},\dots,\Psi_{{\rm LL},8}$; $\Psi^\pm_{\rm LL}$; $\bar\Psi_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | the eight components of $\Psi_{\rm LL}$: components 1 and 2 are the first Dirac component with isospin up and down, 3 and 4 the second, 5 and 6 the third, 7 and 8 the fourth; $\Psi^-_{\rm LL}$ (components 1 to 4, chirality $-1$) and $\Psi^+_{\rm LL}$ (components 5 to 8, chirality $+1$) are the two Weyl isodoublets; $\bar\Psi_{\rm LL}=\Psi^\dagger_{\rm LL}(\gamma^0_{\rm LL}\otimes\sigma^{\rm LL}_0)$ is the Dirac adjoint                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | `Psi_LL^+` `Psi_LL^-`                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| $\psi^{\rm p}_{\rm LL}$, $\psi^{\rm h}_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | the particle and the hole component (eight complex functions each, in the order of the components of $\Psi_{\rm LL}$) of a mode of the mean-field equations: $\psi^{\rm p}_{\rm LL}$ transforms like $\Psi_{\rm LL}$ and $\psi^{\rm h}_{\rm LL}$ like $\Psi^*_{\rm LL}$; a component is written $\psi^{\rm p}_{{\rm LL},k}$, and a numbered mode $(\psi^{\rm p}_{{\rm LL},n},\psi^{\rm h}_{{\rm LL},n})$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | `psi_p` `psi_h`                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| $\mathcal H^{\rm DB}_{\rm LL}$, $h^\nabla_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | the Dirac-Bogoliubov-de Gennes operator of the mean-field equations: the $2\times2$ block matrix (blocks of $8\times8$) with the upper left block $h^\nabla_{\rm LL}-i\Gamma^{\rm LL}_0-\mathsf A_{{\rm LL},0}-\mu_{\rm LL}$, the upper right block $\mathsf P_{\rm LL}$, the lower left block $\mathsf P_{\rm LL}^\dagger$ and the lower right block $-(h^\nabla_{\rm LL}-i\Gamma^{\rm LL}_0-\mathsf A_{{\rm LL},0}-\mu_{\rm LL})^*$, with the covariant Dirac Hamiltonian $h^\nabla_{\rm LL}=-i\sum_j\alpha^j_{\rm LL}\nabla^{\rm LL}_j+\beta_{\rm LL}m_{\rm LL}$ ($\alpha^j_{\rm LL}$ and $\beta_{\rm LL}$ acting on the Dirac index); on a plane wave $\exp(i\sum_jq^j_{\rm LL}x^j_{\rm LL})$ with constant fields it is a $16\times16$ matrix $\mathcal H^{\rm DB}_{\rm LL}(\vec q_{\rm LL})$ (plain text `H_DB(q)`), which without gauge fields equals $\mathsf H_{\rm LL}(\vec q_{\rm LL})$                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | `H_DB` `h_nabla`                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| $\hat a^{\rm qp}_{{\rm LL},n}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | the annihilation operator of the quasi-particle of a numbered mode of the mean-field equations (constant in time): $\Psi_{\rm LL}=\sum_n(\psi^{\rm p}_{{\rm LL},n}\hat a^{\rm qp}_{{\rm LL},n}+(\psi^{\rm h}_{{\rm LL},n})^*(\hat a^{\rm qp}_{{\rm LL},n})^\dagger)$; the quasi-particle vacuum is annihilated by every $\hat a^{\rm qp}_{{\rm LL},n}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | `a_qp`                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| $E^{\rm qp}_{{\rm LL},n}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | the energy of a numbered mode of the mean-field equations in a stationary state, an eigenvalue of $\mathcal H^{\rm DB}_{\rm LL}$; the mean-field ground state is the vacuum of the quasi-particles of the modes with $E^{\rm qp}_{{\rm LL},n}>0$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | `E_qp`                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| $\Pi^\iota_{\rm LL}(x_{\rm LL})$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | the local pair operator $\Pi^\iota_{\rm LL}(x_{\rm LL})=\Psi_{\rm LL}(x_{\rm LL})^T\mathsf K^\iota_{\rm LL}\Psi_{\rm LL}(x_{\rm LL})$, which annihilates two spinor charges at the point $x_{\rm LL}$; its integral over space, with the plane waves normalized in a box, is the operator $\Pi^\iota_{\rm LL}$ of the momenta                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | `Pi_iota(x)`                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| $\mathcal J^{{\rm F},p}_{\rm LL}$, $\mathcal J^{\iota,p}_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | the currents that are the sources of the gauge fields, $\mathcal J^{{\rm F},p}_{\rm LL}=g^{\rm F}_{\rm LL}\bar\Psi_{\rm LL}\gamma^p_{\rm LL}\Psi_{\rm LL}$ and $\mathcal J^{\iota,p}_{\rm LL}=g^{\rm v}_{\rm LL}\bar\Psi_{\rm LL}\gamma^p_{\rm LL}T^\iota_{\rm LL}\Psi_{\rm LL}$ ($\gamma^p_{\rm LL}$ acting on the Dirac index)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | `JF_LL` `Jv_LL`                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| $\mathcal L^\nabla_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | the gauge-invariant Lagrangian density of Test 2: the contact form $\mathcal L^{\rm c}_{\rm LL}$ with the covariant derivative $\nabla^{\rm LL}_p$ in place of $\partial_p$, plus the Lagrangian density of the gauge fields                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | `Lagr_nabla`                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| $\ell^{\rm x}_{\rm LL}$, $\ell^{\rm t}_{\rm LL}$, $r^{\rm W}_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | the lattice spacing (the same in the three directions), the time step and the coefficient of the Wilson term of the lattice equations                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | `dx` `dt` `r_W`                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| $n^{\rm x}_{\rm LL}$, $n^{\rm y}_{\rm LL}$, $n^{\rm z}_{\rm LL}$; $\vec s_{\rm LL}$; $\vec\delta^j_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | the numbers of lattice sites in the directions $x^1_{\rm LL}$, $x^2_{\rm LL}$, $x^3_{\rm LL}$; a lattice site $\vec s_{\rm LL}=(s^1_{\rm LL},s^2_{\rm LL},s^3_{\rm LL})$, whose integer coordinates $s^j_{\rm LL}$ run from 0 to the number of sites of their direction minus 1, at the point $x^j_{\rm LL}=s^j_{\rm LL}\ell^{\rm x}_{\rm LL}$; and the step to the next site in the direction $j$, the unit vector $\vec\delta^j_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | `nx` `ny` `nz` `site` `delta_j`                                                                                                                                                                         |
| $\mathsf U^{\rm link}_{{\rm LL},j}(\vec s_{\rm LL})$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | the link matrix of the lattice from the site $\vec s_{\rm LL}$ to the site $\vec s_{\rm LL}+\vec\delta^j_{\rm LL}$, the $2\times2$ isospin matrix $\exp(-i\ell^{\rm x}_{\rm LL}(g^{\rm v}_{\rm LL}\mathsf a_{{\rm LL},j}+g^{\rm F}_{\rm LL}A^{\rm F}_{{\rm LL},j}\sigma^{\rm LL}_0))$ of the potentials at the site: the parallel transport of the isospin and of the phase over one lattice step                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | `Ulink` `Ulink_j`                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| $\mathcal H^{\rm lat}_{\rm LL}$, $\varepsilon^{\rm lat}_{\rm LL}$, $B^{\rm H}_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | the lattice form of $\mathcal H^{\rm DB}_{\rm LL}$ (a matrix of 16 rows per site), the energy of a free particle on the lattice, and the bound on the largest eigenvalue magnitude of $\mathcal H^{\rm lat}_{\rm LL}$ from which the time step is chosen                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | `H_lat` `eps_lat` `Hbound`                                                                                                                                                                              |
| $\mathsf G^{\rm site}_{\rm LL}$, $\mathsf X^{\rm ph}_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | the gauge transformation of the lattice equations, which acts at each site as $\exp(i\theta^{\rm F}_{\rm LL})\mathsf U_{\rm LL}$ on the isospin index of the particle component and as its complex conjugate on the hole component; and the exchange of the particle and the hole component, $\mathsf X^{\rm ph}_{\rm LL}=\begin{pmatrix}0&\mathsf 1_8\\\mathsf 1_8&0\end{pmatrix}$, with which the particle-hole symmetry reads $\mathsf X^{\rm ph}_{\rm LL}(\mathcal H^{\rm DB}_{\rm LL})^*\mathsf X^{\rm ph}_{\rm LL}=-\mathcal H^{\rm DB}_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | `Gsite` `Xph`                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| $\mathsf A^\uparrow_{{\rm LL},p}$, $\mathsf A^\downarrow_{{\rm LL},p}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | the two eigenvalues of $\mathsf A_{{\rm LL},p}$ when the isospin potentials point along the third isospin direction ($A^1_{{\rm LL},p}=A^2_{{\rm LL},p}=0$): $\mathsf A^\uparrow_{{\rm LL},p}=g^{\rm F}_{\rm LL}A^{\rm F}_{{\rm LL},p}+\tfrac12g^{\rm v}_{\rm LL}A^3_{{\rm LL},p}$ on the isospin-up components ($Q_{\rm LL}=+\tfrac12$) and $\mathsf A^\downarrow_{{\rm LL},p}=g^{\rm F}_{\rm LL}A^{\rm F}_{{\rm LL},p}-\tfrac12g^{\rm v}_{\rm LL}A^3_{{\rm LL},p}$ on the isospin-down components ($Q_{\rm LL}=-\tfrac12$); constant potentials of a space direction shift the momentum of a component, those of the time direction its energy                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | `Acal_up` `Acal_down` `Acal_up_0` `Acal_up_1` `Acal_down_0` `Acal_down_1`                                                                                                                               |
| $\omega^{\rm pr}_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | the angular frequency with which the isospin of a mode turns in a constant isospin potential of the time direction, $\omega^{\rm pr}_{\rm LL}=g^{\rm v}_{\rm LL}\big(\sum_\iota(A^\iota_{{\rm LL},0})^2\big)^{1/2}$; the axis of the rotation is the direction of the potential in isospin space                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | `omega_pr`                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| $P^\uparrow_{\rm LL}$, $P^{\rm h}_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | the probability of isospin up of a mode, the fraction of its norm $\int d^3\vec x_{\rm LL}(\lvert\psi^{\rm p}_{\rm LL}\rvert^2+\lvert\psi^{\rm h}_{\rm LL}\rvert^2)$ that lies in the isospin-up components, and the probability of the hole, the fraction that lies in $\psi^{\rm h}_{\rm LL}$; both are functions of the time $x^0_{\rm LL}$ (in the data files the suffix `_rk4` marks the values of the integration of the lattice equations and `_exact` those of the exact solution)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | `P_up` `P_up_rk4` `P_up_exact` `P_hole` `P_hole_rk4` `P_hole_exact`                                                                                                                                     |
| $\chi^{\rm pw}_{\rm LL}$, $\vartheta^{\rm B}_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | the constant 8-component spinor (Dirac index and isospin) of a plane-wave solution, a normalized eigenvector of $\mathsf h_{\rm LL}(\vec q_{\rm LL})$ or of its lattice form; and the Bogoliubov angle of a plane-wave mode of the uniform polar superfluid, $\cos^2\vartheta^{\rm B}_{\rm LL}=\tfrac12\big(1+(\pm\varepsilon_{\rm LL}-\mu_{\rm LL})/E^\mp_{\rm LL}\big)$ for an eigenvector of the energy $\pm\varepsilon_{\rm LL}$, which divides the mode into its particle part $\cos\vartheta^{\rm B}_{\rm LL}\,\chi^{\rm pw}_{\rm LL}$ and its hole part $\sin\vartheta^{\rm B}_{\rm LL}\,\mathsf K^3_{\rm LL}\chi^{\rm pw}_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | `chi_pw` `theta_B`                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| $\Delta^\infty_{\rm LL}$, $\ell^{\rm K}_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | the value of the gap far from a domain wall (kink) of the pair field, and the width of the wall: the kink $d^\iota_{\rm LL}=(0,0,\Delta_{\rm LL}(x^1_{\rm LL}))$ with $\Delta_{\rm LL}(x^1_{\rm LL})=\Delta^\infty_{\rm LL}\tanh(x^1_{\rm LL}/\ell^{\rm K}_{\rm LL})$ changes the sign of the pair field at $x^1_{\rm LL}=0$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | `Delta_inf` `ell_K`                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| $\varphi^{\rm K}_{\rm LL}$, $\chi^{\rm K}_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | the profile and the constant spinor of a bound state of a kink of the pair field: $\varphi^{\rm K}_{\rm LL}(x^1_{\rm LL})$ solves $d\varphi^{\rm K}_{\rm LL}/dx^1_{\rm LL}=-\Delta_{\rm LL}(x^1_{\rm LL})\varphi^{\rm K}_{\rm LL}$, so that $\varphi^{\rm K}_{\rm LL}=\cosh(x^1_{\rm LL}/\ell^{\rm K}_{\rm LL})^{-\Delta^\infty_{\rm LL}\ell^{\rm K}_{\rm LL}}$ for the kink of the width $\ell^{\rm K}_{\rm LL}$; $\chi^{\rm K}_{\rm LL}$ is a constant 8-component spinor with $\beta_{\rm LL}\chi^{\rm K}_{\rm LL}=\pm\chi^{\rm K}_{\rm LL}$; the bound state is $(\psi^{\rm p}_{\rm LL},\psi^{\rm h}_{\rm LL})=(\varphi^{\rm K}_{\rm LL}\chi^{\rm K}_{\rm LL},\,i(\mathsf K^3_{\rm LL})^\dagger\alpha^1_{\rm LL}\varphi^{\rm K}_{\rm LL}\chi^{\rm K}_{\rm LL})$, with the energy $\pm m_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | `phi_K` `chi_K`                                                                                                                                                                                         |
