| $\mathsf Q^{\rm K}_{\rm LL}$; $k^{\rm K}_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | the constant Hermitian $16\times16$ matrix $\mathsf Q^{\rm K}_{\rm LL}=\begin{pmatrix}0&-i\alpha^1_{\rm LL}(\mathsf K^3_{\rm LL})^\dagger\\-i\alpha^1_{\rm LL}\mathsf K^3_{\rm LL}&0\end{pmatrix}$ (blocks of $8\times8$), with $(\mathsf Q^{\rm K}_{\rm LL})^2$ the unit matrix and the trace 0, through which the square of the Dirac-Bogoliubov-de Gennes operator of a wall of the pair field depends on the slope of the gap: $(\mathcal H^{\rm DB}_{\rm LL})^2=\big(-d^2/d(x^1_{\rm LL})^2+(q^2_{\rm LL})^2+(q^3_{\rm LL})^2+m_{\rm LL}^2+\Delta_{\rm LL}^2\big)+(d\Delta_{\rm LL}/dx^1_{\rm LL})\,\mathsf Q^{\rm K}_{\rm LL}$; and the number $k^{\rm K}_{\rm LL}=0,1,2,\dots$ of a bound state of a kink, below $\Delta^\infty_{\rm LL}\ell^{\rm K}_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | `Qmat_K` `k_K`                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| $E^{\rm R}_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | the Rayleigh quotient of a trial mode on the lattice: the expectation value of $\mathcal H^{\rm lat}_{\rm LL}$ in the mode divided by the norm of the mode; it approximates the eigenvalue nearest to it with an error of second order in the residual of the mode                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | `E_R`                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| $B^\uparrow_{\rm LL}$, $B^\downarrow_{\rm LL}$; $n^{\rm L}_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | the magnetic fields that the isospin-up and the isospin-down components feel in the constant field strengths $\mathsf F^{\rm F}_{{\rm LL},12}$ (of the phase group) and $\mathsf F^3_{{\rm LL},12}$ (of the third isospin direction), $B^\uparrow_{\rm LL}=g^{\rm F}_{\rm LL}\mathsf F^{\rm F}_{{\rm LL},12}+\tfrac12g^{\rm v}_{\rm LL}\mathsf F^3_{{\rm LL},12}$ and $B^\downarrow_{\rm LL}=g^{\rm F}_{\rm LL}\mathsf F^{\rm F}_{{\rm LL},12}-\tfrac12g^{\rm v}_{\rm LL}\mathsf F^3_{{\rm LL},12}$ (the fields point along $x^3_{\rm LL}$); and the number $n^{\rm L}_{\rm LL}=0,1,2,\dots$ of a Landau level, of the energies $\pm\big(m_{\rm LL}^2+(q^3_{\rm LL})^2+2n^{\rm L}_{\rm LL}\lvert B^\uparrow_{\rm LL}\rvert\big)^{1/2}$ for isospin up and likewise with $B^\downarrow_{\rm LL}$ for isospin down                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | `B_up` `B_down` `n_L`                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| $n^{\rm act}_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | the number of active directions of a lattice problem, the directions with more than one site (1, 2 or 3); the mode functions of a problem with $n^{\rm act}_{\rm LL}$ active directions are normalized to $\sum_{\vec s_{\rm LL}}(\ell^{\rm x}_{\rm LL})^{n^{\rm act}_{\rm LL}}(\lvert\psi^{\rm p}_{\rm LL}\rvert^2+\lvert\psi^{\rm h}_{\rm LL}\rvert^2)=1$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | `n_act`                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| $N^{\rm F}_{\rm LL}$, $E^{\rm MF}_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | the fermion number of a self-consistent lattice state, the sum over its modes and over the sites of the squared hole components $\lvert\psi^{\rm h}_{\rm LL}\rvert^2$ (conserved by the self-consistent evolution), and the mean-field energy functional, the sum over the modes of the expectation values of $\mathcal H^{\rm lat}_{\rm LL}$ without the pair field plus $2(\ell^{\rm x}_{\rm LL})^{n^{\rm act}_{\rm LL}}/G_{\rm LL}$ times the sum over the sites of $\sum_\iota\lvert d^\iota_{\rm LL}\rvert^2$ (conserved in time-independent gauge fields)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | `N_F` `E_MF`                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| $\mathsf W^{\rm m}_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | the matrix whose columns are the modes of a self-consistent lattice state (unit vectors with 16 components per site); the self-consistent evolution moves all columns together                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | `W_modes`                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| $\mathcal M^{\rm sc}_{\rm LL}$; $y^{\rm A}_{\rm LL}$, $r^{\rm A}_{\rm LL}$, $Y^{\rm A}_{\rm LL}$, $R^{\rm A}_{\rm LL}$, $c^{\rm A}_{\rm LL}$, $a^{\rm A}_{\rm LL}$; $n^{\rm it}_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                       | the self-consistency map, which takes a pair field to $-\tfrac12G_{\rm LL}$ times the pair expectation of the positive-energy eigenvectors of $\mathcal H^{\rm lat}_{\rm LL}$ with that pair field, divided by $(\ell^{\rm x}_{\rm LL})^{n^{\rm act}_{\rm LL}}$; the quantities of the Anderson iteration for its fixed point: the iterate (the real and imaginary parts of all $d^\iota_{\rm LL}$ on all sites, as one real vector), its residual (the map applied to the iterate, minus the iterate), the matrices whose columns are the differences of successive iterates and of successive residuals, the coefficients of the least-squares combination, the damping factor, and the number of the iteration                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | `M_sc` `y_A` `r_A` `Y_A` `R_A` `c_A` `a_A` `n_it`                                                                                                                                                       |
| $\vec x^{\rm c}_{\rm LL}$, $w^{\rm pk}_{\rm LL}$, $\vec q^{\rm pk}_{\rm LL}$, $\chi^{\rm pk}_{\rm LL}$, $s^{\rm br}_{\rm LL}$, $\mathsf Z^\pm_{\rm LL}(\vec q_{\rm LL})$                                                                                                                                                                        | the Gaussian wave packet of the solvers: its centre, its width (the standard deviation of its density), its central momentum, its 8-component spinor (the projection of a reference spinor of definite spin and isospin on the chosen branch at the central momentum), the sign $\pm1$ of the chosen branch $\pm\varepsilon^{\rm lat}_{\rm LL}$, and the projectors $\tfrac12(\mathsf 1_4\pm\mathsf h^{\rm lat}_{\rm LL}(\vec q_{\rm LL})/\varepsilon^{\rm lat}_{\rm LL}(\vec q_{\rm LL}))$ on the two branches of the free lattice operator at the momentum $\vec q_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | `x_c` `w_pk` `q_pk` `chi_pk` `s_br` `Zproj`                                                                                                                                                             |
| $\mathsf h^{\rm lat}_{\rm LL}(\vec q_{\rm LL})$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | the $4\times4$ matrix of the free lattice operator (without isospin) on the plane wave $\exp(i\vec q_{\rm LL}\cdot\vec x_{\rm LL})$: $\sum_j\alpha^j_{\rm LL}\sin(q^j_{\rm LL}\ell^{\rm x}_{\rm LL})/\ell^{\rm x}_{\rm LL}+\beta_{\rm LL}\big(m_{\rm LL}+(r^{\rm W}_{\rm LL}/\ell^{\rm x}_{\rm LL})\sum_j(1-\cos(q^j_{\rm LL}\ell^{\rm x}_{\rm LL}))\big)$, the sums over the active directions; its square is $\varepsilon^{\rm lat}_{\rm LL}(\vec q_{\rm LL})^2$ times $\mathsf 1_4$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | `h_lat_q`                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| $\vec v^{\rm g}_{\rm LL}$, $\bar v^{\rm g}_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | the group velocity, the gradient of $\varepsilon^{\rm lat}_{\rm LL}$ with respect to $\vec q_{\rm LL}$, and its mean over the distribution of momenta of a wave packet                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | `v_g` `v_g_mean`                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| $\ell^{\rm B}_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | the magnetic length $\lvert B^\uparrow_{\rm LL}\rvert^{-1/2}$ of the isospin-up component in a constant magnetic field                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | `l_B`                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| $G'_{\rm LL}$; $\bar\Delta_{\rm LL}$; $\omega^{\rm H}_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | the coupling after a quench (the coupling is changed from $G_{\rm LL}$ to $G'_{\rm LL}$ at $x^0_{\rm LL}=0$); the mean over the time of the gap $\lvert\vec d_{\rm LL}\rvert$ after the quench; and the angular frequency of the amplitude oscillation of the pair field (the amplitude mode, the analogue of the Higgs boson)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | `G_quench` `Delta_mean` `omega_H`                                                                                                                                                                       |
| $u^{\rm Bl}_{\rm LL}$, $v^{\rm Bl}_{\rm LL}$; $\vec b^{\rm Bl}_{\rm LL}$, $\vec h^{\rm Bl}_{\rm LL}$; $\vec b^{\rm fl}_{\rm LL}$, $\Delta^{\rm fl}_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                    | the two complex coefficients of a state of the plane (3.12) (of the particle vector and of the hole vector that span it); the Bloch vector of that state, the expectation values of $\sigma^{\rm LL}_1$, $\sigma^{\rm LL}_2$, $\sigma^{\rm LL}_3$ in it, and the vector of the coefficients of the $2\times2$ matrix (3.12) in the Pauli matrices, about which it precesses; their small deviations from the ground state in the linearized equations: the deviation of the Bloch vector and of the gap                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | `u_Bl` `v_Bl` `b_Bl` `h_Bl` `b_fl` `Delta_fl`                                                                                                                                                           |
| $\mathsf K^{\rm g}_{\rm LL}$, $\mathsf K^{\rm g'}_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | two general pair matrices: complex $8\times8$ matrices with which $\Psi^T_{\rm LL}\mathsf K^{\rm g}_{\rm LL}\Psi_{\rm LL}=\sum_{k,k'}(\mathsf K^{\rm g}_{\rm LL})_{kk'}\Psi_{{\rm LL},k}\Psi_{{\rm LL},k'}$ annihilates two spinor charges at one point; only the antisymmetric part of a pair matrix contributes, because the components of $\Psi_{\rm LL}$ anticommute                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | `Kgen` `Kgen’`                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| $\mathsf V^\pm_{\rm LL}$; $\mathsf B^{\rm W}_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | the isospin factors of the two Weyl blocks of a Lorentz-invariant pair matrix, which is $\mathrm{diag}(\mathsf E_{\rm LL}\otimes\mathsf V^-_{\rm LL},\mathsf E_{\rm LL}\otimes\mathsf V^+_{\rm LL})$ with two symmetric complex $2\times2$ matrices $\mathsf V^\pm_{\rm LL}$ on the isospin index; and a complex $2\times2$ matrix on the Weyl index, the Weyl factor of a term of a $4\times4$ block of a pair matrix (in the proof that the Lorentz-invariant pairs have this form)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | `Vblock` `BW`                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| $\mathsf K^{(+1)}_{\rm LL}$, $\mathsf K^{(0)}_{\rm LL}$, $\mathsf K^{(-1)}_{\rm LL}$; $\Pi^{(+1)}_{\rm LL}$, $\Pi^{(0)}_{\rm LL}$, $\Pi^{(-1)}_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                        | the scalar pair matrices of definite electric charge, $\mathsf K^{(\pm1)}_{\rm LL}=(\mathsf K^1_{\rm LL}\mp i\mathsf K^2_{\rm LL})/\sqrt2$ and $\mathsf K^{(0)}_{\rm LL}=\mathsf K^3_{\rm LL}$, and the pair operators $\Pi^{(+1)}_{\rm LL}$, $\Pi^{(0)}_{\rm LL}$, $\Pi^{(-1)}_{\rm LL}$ made with them, which annihilate a pair of the electric charge $+1$ (two isospin-up charges), $0$ (one isospin-up and one isospin-down charge) and $-1$ (two isospin-down charges)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | `K(+1)` `K(0)` `K(-1)` `Pi(+1)` `Pi(0)` `Pi(-1)`                                                                                                                                                        |
| $S^{\rm sp}_{\rm LL}$, $I^{\rm v}_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | the spin and the isospin of a state of two spinor charges: the sums of the squares of the three total spin generators and of the three total isospin generators have the eigenvalues $S^{\rm sp}_{\rm LL}(S^{\rm sp}_{\rm LL}+1)$ and $I^{\rm v}_{\rm LL}(I^{\rm v}_{\rm LL}+1)$; the superfluid boson singlet has $S^{\rm sp}_{\rm LL}=0$ (spin singlet) and $I^{\rm v}_{\rm LL}=1$ (isospin triplet)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | `S_sp` `I_v`                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| $\hat c_{{\rm LL},k}(\vec s_{\rm LL})$; $\hat\Pi^\iota_{\rm LL}(\vec s_{\rm LL})$, $\hat n^{\rm F}_{\rm LL}(\vec s_{\rm LL})$, $\hat n^\iota_{\rm LL}(\vec s_{\rm LL})$                                                                                                                                                                         | operators of one lattice site $\vec s_{\rm LL}$ (a cell of the volume $(\ell^{\rm x}_{\rm LL})^3$): the annihilation operators $\hat c_{{\rm LL},k}(\vec s_{\rm LL})=(\ell^{\rm x}_{\rm LL})^{3/2}\Psi_{{\rm LL},k}(\vec s_{\rm LL})$ of the eight components, which obey the canonical anticommutation relations; the pair operator of the site $\hat\Pi^\iota_{\rm LL}=\sum_{k,k'}(\mathsf K^\iota_{\rm LL})_{kk'}\hat c_{{\rm LL},k}\hat c_{{\rm LL},k'}$; the number of spinor charges on the site $\hat n^{\rm F}_{\rm LL}=\sum_k\hat c^\dagger_{{\rm LL},k}\hat c_{{\rm LL},k}$; and the isospin of the site $\hat n^\iota_{\rm LL}=\sum_{k,k'}\hat c^\dagger_{{\rm LL},k}(T^\iota_{\rm LL})_{kk'}\hat c_{{\rm LL},k'}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | `c_site` `Pi_site` `n_F_site` `n_iota_site`                                                                                                                                                             |
| $\hat H^{\rm q}_{\rm LL}$; $\lvert\Phi^{\rm SF}_{\rm LL}\rangle$, $\lvert\Phi^{\rm N}_{\rm LL}\rangle$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | the part of the mean-field Hamiltonian of the uniform polar state that belongs to one pair of momenta $\vec q_{\rm LL}$, $-\vec q_{\rm LL}$ (an operator on 16 fermion modes); the superfluid boson singlet state, its ground state, a coherent state of pairs; and the normal state from which it is built, the Dirac sea of these modes (every state of energy $-\varepsilon_{\rm LL}$ filled, every state of energy $+\varepsilon_{\rm LL}$ empty)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | `H_q` `SF_state` `Dirac_sea`                                                                                                                                                                            |
| $\hat B^{\rm p}_{\rm LL}(\vec q_{\rm LL})$, $\hat B^{\rm a}_{\rm LL}(\vec q_{\rm LL})$; $t^{\rm p}_{\rm LL}$, $t^{\rm a}_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                              | the pair operators of the momenta $\vec q_{\rm LL}$ and $-\vec q_{\rm LL}$ in the scalar isospin channel of $\mathsf K^3_{\rm LL}$: $\hat B^{\rm p}_{\rm LL}(\vec q_{\rm LL})^\dagger$ creates a pair of spinor charges of positive energy (particles), $\hat B^{\rm a}_{\rm LL}(\vec q_{\rm LL})^\dagger$ removes a pair from the states of negative energy (creates a pair of antiparticles); and their weights $t^{\rm p}_{\rm LL}=\Delta_{\rm LL}/(E^-_{\rm LL}+\varepsilon_{\rm LL}-\mu_{\rm LL})$ and $t^{\rm a}_{\rm LL}=\Delta_{\rm LL}/(E^+_{\rm LL}+\varepsilon_{\rm LL}+\mu_{\rm LL})$ in the superfluid boson singlet state                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | `B_p` `B_a` `t_p` `t_a`                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| $\mathsf Y^\pm_{\rm LL}(\vec q_{\rm LL})$; $\hat f_{\rm LL}$, $\hat f'_{\rm LL}$                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | the projectors $\tfrac12(\mathsf 1_8\pm\mathsf h_{\rm LL}(\vec q_{\rm LL})/\varepsilon_{\rm LL})$ on the states of energy $\pm\varepsilon_{\rm LL}$ of the plane-wave Hamiltonian (rank 4 each); and the annihilation operators of a spinor charge in an eigenstate of $\mathsf h_{\rm LL}(\vec q_{\rm LL})$ and in its partner state of the momentum $-\vec q_{\rm LL}$ (the complex conjugate of $\mathsf K^3_{\rm LL}$ times the eigenvector)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | `Yproj` `f_state` `f_partner`                                                                                                                                                                           |

The symbols of this paper, by group, with the plain-text names under which the programs, the figures and the data files write them.

*Plain-text names.* The programs, the figures and the data files write formulas in plain text, with the names of the last column of Table III. The following conventions complete that column.

- Elements of the algebras: `x`, `y`, `z`, `w` are the bi-octonions $\mathbf x$, $\mathbf y$, $\mathbf z$, $\mathbf w$; `aH`, `bS`, `cH`, `dS`, `bS’` are the bi-quaternions $\mathbf a$, $\mathbf b$, $\mathbf c$, $\mathbf d$, $\mathbf b'$ of the form $\mathbf a+\mathbf b\,\mathbf e_4$ (the letter H recalls that the element lies in $\mathbb
  C\otimes\mathbb H$, the letter S that it multiplies $\mathbf e_4$ to give a spinor charge); `uL`, `uL’`, `uL1`, `uL2` are $\mathbf u$, $\mathbf u'$, $\mathbf u_1$, $\mathbf u_2$ (Lorentz part); `tI`, `tI’` are $\mathbf t$, $\mathbf t'$ (isospin); `gL`, `hL` are $\mathbf g$, $\mathbf h$ (elements of $SL(2,\mathbb C)$); `vI` is $\mathbf v$ (a unit quaternion, an isospin rotation); `rP`, `rP’` are $\mathbf r$, $\mathbf r'$ (translations). `e0`, …, `e7` are the units $\mathbf e_0,\dots,\mathbf
  e_7$ (and `1` in `gL = 1 + i e1 + e2` is $\mathbf e_0$); `ie1`, …, `ie7` are $i\mathbf e_1,\dots,i\mathbf e_7$; `e_p`, `e_j`, `e_iota` are $\mathbf e_p$, $\mathbf e_j$, $\mathbf e_\iota$.

- Operations: a final `bar` (`xbar`, `bSbar`, `gLbar`) is the conjugation $\bar{\mathbf x}$; `^*` the complex conjugation; `^dag` the Hermitian conjugation; `^T` the transpose; `^-1` the inverse; `(x)` the tensor product $\otimes$; `o` the composition $\circ$; `[x,y]` the commutator and `[x,y,z]` the associator; `Jac` the Jacobiator; `L_x`, `R_x` the left and right multiplications $L^{\rm LL}_{\mathbf x}$, $R^{\rm LL}_{\mathbf x}$, and `L_[e1,e4]` the left multiplication by the commutator $[\mathbf e_1,\mathbf e_4]$; `cross(a, b)` is the cross product of two complex three-component vectors; `|...|` an absolute value or the length of a vector; the dot in `kE.(kE+pE)` the Euclidean scalar product; `x -> y` a map; `x(y)` a product or a function value, as the context of the formula shows.

- Coefficients of the families: `c_tw`, `c_sl`, `c_PP`, `c_Ps`, `c_sP`, `c_TT`, `c_Ts`, `c_sT` are $c^{\rm tw}_{\rm LL}$, …, $c^{\rm sT}_{\rm LL}$; a complex one is split as `ctw_re + i ctw_im` into its real and imaginary parts (and so for the others); `csP` and `cTT` are real. In checks M-3, M-4, M-7 and M-9 a failing triple or 4-tuple is given by the positions of its elements in the basis of the family: positions 0, 1, 2 are $\mathbf e_1$, $\mathbf e_2$, $\mathbf e_3$ and 3, 4, 5 are $i\mathbf e_1$, $i\mathbf e_2$, $i\mathbf e_3$ (the Lorentz part); in $\mathcal F^{\rm B}_{\rm LL}$ positions 6 to 9 are the translations $\mathbf e_0$, $i\mathbf e_1$, $i\mathbf e_2$, $i\mathbf e_3$, and in $\mathcal F^{\rm C}_{\rm
  LL}$ positions 6 to 8 are the isospin elements $\mathbf e_1$, $\mathbf e_2$, $\mathbf e_3$; the last eight positions are the spinor charges $\mathbf e_4,\dots,\mathbf e_7$, $i\mathbf e_4,\dots,i\mathbf e_7$.

- The superfluid: `dvec` is $\vec d_{\rm LL}$ and `d_iota` its components; `eps2` is $\mathsf E_{\rm LL}$, `eps3` the Levi-Civita symbol; `E^(+-)` stands for $E^+_{\rm LL}$ and $E^-_{\rm LL}$, and `E^(+-)(Delta)` for these energies with the gap `Delta` in place of $\Delta_{\rm LL}$; `Delta_(+-)` are the gaps $\Delta^\pm_{\rm LL}$; `Etilde^(+-)` are $\tilde E^\pm_{\rm LL}$; `eps` is $\varepsilon_{\rm LL}$, `q` the momentum $\vec q_{\rm LL}$ or its length; `Q_LL(Kch)` is the electric charge of the two spinor charges that the pair operator $\Psi^T_{\rm LL}\mathsf K\Psi_{\rm LL}$ annihilates; `0+` and `0-` are spin zero with positive and negative parity; `spin(spin+1)` in check Q-3 is the spin quantum number times itself plus one, the value 0, 2 or $\tfrac34$ of the Casimir for the spins 0, 1 and $\tfrac12$.

- Abbreviations: `BdG` is Bogoliubov-de Gennes, `BCS` Bardeen-Cooper-Schrieffer; `M_2(C)` the complex $2\times2$ matrices; `R^8`, `C^3`, `C^8` the real or complex vector spaces of that dimension; `Im H`, `Im O` and `C (x) H` are ${\rm Im}\,\mathbb H$, ${\rm Im}\,\mathbb O$ and $\mathbb C\otimes\mathbb H$; the groups and algebras (`SL(2,C)`, `so(3,1)`, `g2(C)`, `SU(2)_v`, `U(1)_F_LL`, `Z2`, …) are written as in the text; `4x4` means four by four; “exact” in a check means integer or rational arithmetic, “double precision” floating-point arithmetic with 53-bit mantissas.

In the comments of the source code the quaternion units $\mathbf e_1$, $\mathbf e_2$, $\mathbf e_3$ are also called `i_q`, `j_q`, `k_q`, and the unit $\mathbf e_4$ of the Cayley-Dickson construction `l`; these names do not occur in the reports, the data files or the figures.

## Appendix B: The machine checks

The program `superloop` (Appendix C) verifies 62 statements, the script `malcev_groebner.py` the 12 statements G-1 to G-12 of the classification; the script `check_symbols.py` verifies the eight statements LL-S1 to LL-S8 on the symbols (Appendix A), and `check_self_contained.py` the statement LL-H1, that no document of this revision sends its reader to another document for an equation, a symbol or a definition. Each check prints one line: its label, the statement, and, after two dashes, the numbers that decide it. A statement marked “exact” is decided in exact integer or rational arithmetic, one marked “double precision” with a tolerance far above the rounding errors and far below the effect that it tests. Table IV reproduces these lines as the programs print them, in plain text (Appendix A explains the names).

|:------|:-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|:-------|

| A-2   | B_LL is NOT associative: exactly 168 of the 512 ordered triples of units have a non-zero associator (the triples of distinct imaginary units outside the 7 quaternionic triples); example \[e1,e2,e4\] = (e1 e2) e4 - e1 (e2 e4) = 2 e7 – non-zero associators: 168; coefficients of \[e1,e2,e4\] on e0..e7: \[0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 2\]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | PASS   |
| A-3   | on 300 random bi-octonions x, y, z with Gaussian-integer coefficients the complex norm is multiplicative, N_LL(xy) = N_LL(x) N_LL(y), and the three Moufang identities z(x(zy)) = ((zx)z)y, ((yz)x)z = y(z(xz)), (zx)(yz) = (z(xy))z hold exactly: the unit bi-octonions form a Moufang LOOP, the super-loop L_LL                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | PASS   |

| LL-S5 | in the 5329 formulas of the documents, mu, Lambda, Omega, nu, Delta, zeta and varepsilon always carry the suffix LL                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | PASS   |
| LL-S6 | in the 6281 formulas of the documents and of the new registry entries, no Latin letter except the index j, D, the imaginary unit i, the index p and the differential d stands without the suffix LL in its scripts, and the Greek letters that are symbols carry the suffix as well                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | PASS   |
| LL-S7 | every one of the 4616 occurrences of a letter in bold, sans-serif, calligraphic, blackboard or fraktur type in the formulas of the documents is a registered symbol (67 registered styled letters)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | PASS   |
| LL-S8 | every one of the 3625 occurrences of a name with the suffix \_LL, of an algebra element, of a family coefficient or of a pair matrix in the 115 reports and data files (data/sl\_\*, explanations/data/ex\_\* and test2/data/t2\_\*) is a registered plain-text name                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | PASS   |
| LL-S9 | the 2042 formulas of the 1 built documents of the folder test2, with the explicit matrices and equations written by the programs inserted, satisfy LL-S5, LL-S6 and LL-S7                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | PASS   |

All machine checks of this paper, as the programs print them.

## Appendix C: Programs, data, and how to reproduce every number

### A. Programs

All computations except the Gröbner bases are done by the Rust program `superloop` in the folder `code/superloop-rs` of `Revision_SuperLoop` (9 source files, 3305 lines); the Gröbner bases are computed by the Python script `code/scripts/malcev_groebner.py` (486 lines) with Buchberger’s algorithm over the rationals, as implemented in the computer-algebra library SymPy. The figures are drawn by the Python script `code/scripts/make_figures.py` from the data files, and the documents are assembled by Python scripts that insert every number from the data files, so that no computed number is typed by hand.

The program has nine modules. `bioct`: quaternions with complex coefficients and the Cayley-Dickson product of Eq. (4) (and, with the opposite sign of the term $\bar{\mathbf
d}\mathbf b$, the split octonions), the conjugations, the norm $N_{\rm LL}$, the commutator, the associator, the Jacobiator and the linearized Malcev expression (8), the left and right multiplications as real $16\times16$ integer matrices, and a deterministic pseudo-random generator (SplitMix64) for the random tests; the coefficients are exact Gaussian integers (real and imaginary parts are 128-bit integers, and an overflow stops the program) or double-precision complex numbers. `exact`: exact linear algebra on integer matrices—spans and ranks over the rationals by fraction-free Gaussian elimination (each row divided by the greatest common divisor of its entries), Lie closures (commutators are added until the exact span stops growing), the signature of a symmetric matrix by symmetric elimination in exact rational arithmetic, and ranks modulo the prime $2^{61}-1$ for the large systems (a rank modulo a prime can only be smaller than the rank over the rationals, so the dimension of a solution space computed from it is an upper bound; each such bound is matched by the same number of explicitly verified solutions). `algebra` (command `algebra`: checks A, L, Q and C), `malcev` (command `malcev`: the brackets of Eq. (20) with integer or Gaussian-integer coefficients, the Jacobi identity on all basis triples and the linearized Malcev identity on all basis 4-tuples, $14^4=38416$ for $\mathcal F^{\rm A}_{\rm LL}$, $18^4=104976$ for $\mathcal F^{\rm B}_{\rm LL}$ and $17^4=83521$ for $\mathcal F^{\rm C}_{\rm LL}$; checks M), `manifolds` (command `manifolds`: checks N), `superfluid` (command `superfluid`: the Dirac matrices, the pair channels, the Bogoliubov-de Gennes matrices, the grand potential and the gap equation; checks S), `renorm` (command `renorm`: power counting, the contact coupling, the bubble, the invariants; checks R), `checks` (the reports) and `main` (the commands, and a guard that lets the program run only in the folder `Revision_SuperLoop`, into whose folder `data` it writes). The library crate `code/spacetimes-rs` of the repository supplies complex numbers, the diagonalization of complex Hermitian matrices, Gauss-Legendre nodes and weights, Brent’s root finder and the writing of data files.

*Numerical methods.* The statements of the checks A, L, C, M, N, Q-3, Q-4, Q-6, R-1, R-2, R-5 and R-6 are decided in exact integer or rational arithmetic. The other checks use double precision: the eigenvalues of checks Q-1, Q-2 and Q-5; the structural checks of the superfluid, S-0, S-1, S-2, S-7, S-8 and S-9, whose matrices have the entries 0, $\pm1$, $\pm i$ and $\pm\tfrac12$, so that their products are exact or rounded at the level $10^{-15}$, compared with tolerances between $10^{-14}$ and $10^{-10}$ (a rank is counted as the number of eigenvalues of the Gram matrix of the vectors that exceed $10^{-10}$ times the largest); the spectra of check S-3, from the diagonalization of the $16\times16$ Bogoliubov-de Gennes matrices at 60 random points, with random momenta, masses, chemical potentials and order parameters; and the integrals of checks S-4 to S-6, S-10, R-3 and R-4. The momentum integrals of Sec. IX use Gauss-Legendre rules of 40 points on panels whose end points are 0, the cutoff, and 0.5, 0.9, 0.99, 1, 1.01, 1.1 and 1.5 times the Fermi momentum $k^{\rm
F}_{\rm LL}$ (where these lie inside the cutoff); those of check R-3 use rules of 24 points with end points at 0.5, 0.9, 0.99, 1, 1.01, 1.1 and 2 times $k^{\rm F}_{\rm LL}$ and further panels whose lengths double up to the cutoff. The gap equation (29) is solved for $\Delta_{\rm LL}$ by Brent’s method between $10^{-9}$ and 20 with the tolerance $10^{-13}$; the minimum of $\Omega_{\rm LL}$ over $\lvert\vec d_{\rm LL}\rvert$ is found by golden-section search (120 or 150 steps) on an interval from 0 to three times the gap. The slope of check S-5 is taken from the two weakest couplings of the grid of 40 couplings, the slope of check R-4 from the two largest cutoffs. The bubble (36) is integrated in four-dimensional spherical coordinates: the length of the loop momentum on panels whose lengths grow by the factor 1.5 from 0.25 up to the cutoff, and the angle between the loop momentum and the external momentum from 0 to $\pi$ with the weight $4\pi$ times the square of its sine (the volume of the three-sphere of directions), each with Gauss-Legendre rules of 48 points.

### B. Commands

From the folder `Revision_SuperLoop` of the repository (a Rust toolchain for the edition 2021, Python 3 with SymPy, NumPy and Matplotlib, and for the documents a TeX distribution with REVTeX 4.2, pandoc and XeLaTeX):

    cargo run --release --manifest-path code/superloop-rs/Cargo.toml -- all
    python3 code/scripts/malcev_groebner.py
    python3 code/scripts/make_figures.py
    bash code/scripts/check_all.sh

The first command writes the data files of the program and the reports of its checks into the folder `data` (in about one second); the commands `algebra`, `malcev`, `manifolds`, `superfluid` and `renorm` in place of `all` run one part. The second computes the Gröbner bases and the checks G-1 to G-12 (a few seconds). The third draws the seven figures. The fourth runs all three, then the checks of the symbols and of self-containedness, writes the fragments of the documents, builds this paper and the provenance documents, and ends with one verdict.

### C. Data files

`sl_associators.csv`: the non-zero associators \[e_p1, e_p2, e_p3\] = (e_p1 e_p2) e_p3 - e_p1 (e_p2 e_p3) of three units with p1 $<$ p2 $<$ p3; each equals coefficient x the unit result (all other orders follow by alternativity). `sl_contact_running.csv`: bare contact coupling G versus the cutoff Lambda at the fixed gap Delta = 0.05 (mu = 0.5, m = 0.2). `sl_malcev_families.csv`: members of the covariant families verified exhaustively: Jacobi on all basis triples, linearized Malcev on all basis 4-tuples. `sl_malcev_structure.csv`: non-zero commutators of the real basis elements of the Malcev algebra m_LL = C (x) Im O: \[left, right\] = coefficient x result (left before right in the basis order e1, e2, e3, ie1, ie2, ie3, e4..e7, ie4..ie7). `sl_manifolds.csv`: manifolds that carry the Lorentz super-loop. `sl_multiplication_table.csv`: products of the units e0..e7 of B_LL (row: left factor, column: right factor); the bi-octonions are the complex combinations of the units. `sl_power_counting.csv`: superficial degree of divergence deg: theory 0 = contact (next_F = 4), theory 1 = Yukawa (next_F = 2, next_B = 0), versus the number of vertices nvert, for D = 3 and D = 7 space dimensions. `sl_quantum_numbers.csv`: quantum numbers of the sectors of B_LL under the operators of the super-loop: Q_LL (eigenvalues with multiplicities), T_LL^2, Cas_LL, Lorentz type, spinor number exp(2 pi D_LL(J_LL^3)), twist charge Theta_LL. `sl_sf_channels.csv`: pair channels of the spinor charges: pair matrix, spin and parity, isospin, charge Q_LL of the pair, remark. `sl_sf_dispersion.csv`: quasiparticle energies versus q at G = 12, mu = 0.5, m = 0.2: polar state E^-, E^+; charged state (zeta = 1) gapped branch E^-(sqrt(2) Delta) \[its other branch is the normal $|$eps - mu$|$\]; normal state $|$eps - mu$|$; pseudoscalar channel Etilde^-, Etilde^+ at the same Delta. `sl_sf_gap_vs_coupling.csv`: unitary scalar (0+) isospin-triplet state: gap Delta and Omega(Delta) - Omega(0) versus the coupling G (cutoff Lambda = 1, mu = 0.5, m = 0.2); G_over_Gcrit_m0 is G divided by G_crit at mu = 0, m = 0. `sl_sf_omega_zeta.csv`: minimum over $|$dvec$|$ of Omega(dvec) - Omega(0) at fixed zeta = $|$cross(dvec, dvec^\*)$|$/$|$dvec$|$^2 (G = 12, mu = 0.5, m = 0.2), the $|$dvec$|$ of the minimum, and Omega at $|$dvec$|$ = the gap of the polar state. `sl_sf_states.csv`: spin-zero isospin-triplet states of the superfluid of the spinor charges: order parameter dvec, zeta, dimension of the stabilizer in SU(2)\_v x U(1)\_F_LL, broken generators, charge of the condensed pairs, gaps. `sl_yukawa_bubble.csv`: Euclidean bubble Ibub of the Yukawa completion versus the cutoff Lambda at pE = 1, m = 0.5; Ibub(0) grows like Lambda^2, Ibub(pE) - Ibub(0) like -(pE^2/16 pi^2) ln Lambda. `sl_groebner_bases.txt`: the reduced Groebner bases of the six constraint ideals. `sl_algebra_checks.txt`, `sl_malcev_checks.txt`, `sl_manifolds_checks.txt`, `sl_superfluid_checks.txt`, `sl_renorm_checks.txt`, `sl_groebner_checks.txt`, `sl_symbol_checks.txt`, `sl_self_contained_checks.txt`: the reports of Table IV. Every data file states its content and units in its header lines (`# content:`), and every column name is either a descriptive word or one of the names listed in the last column of Table III. The reports `sl_algebra_checks.txt`, `sl_malcev_checks.txt`, `sl_manifolds_checks.txt`, `sl_superfluid_checks.txt`, `sl_renorm_checks.txt`, `sl_groebner_checks.txt`, `sl_symbol_checks.txt` and `sl_self_contained_checks.txt` contain the lines of Table IV, and `sl_groebner_bases.txt` the reduced Gröbner bases of all six ideals of Table II.
